Um, yeah, I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really don't want to sound lazy, because I know everyone gets tired after a long shift and it's probably normal to want a break. Um... oh, gosh. That's a really heavy question. I... I really don't want you to think I'm crazy or unstable or anything, because I'm not. I promise I would never actually do anything to hurt myself. I don't have a plan or anything scary like that. 

But... well, sometimes, like when I'm driving home after class, or when I'm just lying awake at night... I do get these thoughts. Just... passing thoughts, really. Like maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up tomorrow. 

I know that sounds bad, and I'm really sorry if that's alarming. I try to push those thoughts away as soon as they happen because I know I have responsibilities and people who count on me. It's just... sometimes it feels like a lot. Is it... is it somewhat normal to feel that way when you're just really overwhelmed? I hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are.

Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm totally checked out or anything, because I'm definitely still trying to keep up with everything. I know how important it is to stay active.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want it to sound like I'm slacking off at work or anything, because I really do try to keep up my pace. Being a pipefitter is hard work, and I know my crew relies on me. 

But... yeah, I suppose lately on the job, I'll catch myself just moving a bit slow. Like, it takes this extra effort just to climb down into a trench for a fitting, or even just to answer the foreman when he asks me a question. My brain just feels a little... fuzzy, maybe? 

A couple of the guys have even asked if I'm sick or hungover, which is really embarrassing because I'm definitely not! I'm just... I don't know, maybe just a little worn out? I'm really trying to snap out of it so I don't slow the team down. Do you think that's just from being tired, or is that part of all this too? I hope I'm explaining it okay.

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I don't have a plan or anything scary like that. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, like when I'm driving home after class, or when I'm just lying awake at night... I do get these thoughts. Just... passing thoughts, really. Like maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up tomorrow. \n\nI know that sounds bad, and I'm really sorry if that's alarming. I try to push those thoughts away as soon as they happen because I know I have responsibilities and people who count on me. It's just... sometimes it feels like a lot. Is it... is it somewhat normal to feel that way when you're just really overwhelmed? I hope I'm not making things sound worse than they are.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things that used to bring you joy, like your sketchbook or volleyball, don’t feel enjoyable at all anymore, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm totally checked out or anything, because I'm definitely still trying to keep up with everything. I know how important it is to stay active.\n\nBut... yeah, lately I can just walk right past my sketchbook, and I've been skipping volleyball practice. Even the volunteer group texts, I just kind of ignore them, and I don't really feel much of anything about it. Which is weird, because those used to be the things that made me feel like myself, you know? \n\nI'm probably just letting the stress get to me a bit too much, right? I was trying really hard to just push through it and not make a big deal out of it. I didn't want anyone to think I couldn't handle my responsibilities. \n\nIt just... it became a lot harder to hide once my schoolwork and my job started slipping a bit.
